97	
98	**UI rules that apply to every WP above (TDD §12.4):**
99	- Use Kumo components first (`Sidebar`, `Button`, `Badge`, `LayerCard`, `Surface`, `Collapsible`, `Code`, `Loader`, `Banner`, `Toast`, `Dialog`, `ClipboardText`, `Input`, `SensitiveInput`, `Select`/`Radio`, `Table`, `Tooltip`, `Empty`).
100	- Never use Tailwind `dark:` variants. Theming goes through `light-dark()` and `data-mode`.
101	- Orange is only for primary CTAs (Send, Approve) and highlight rings. Everything else uses Kumo's neutral variants.
102	- Headings use `font-display` at weight 500. Hostnames, evidence IDs and raw evidence use `font-mono`.
103	- Every control works from the keyboard, and status is never shown by colour alone.

412	| 2026-10-15 19:44 | 13b | **Live speed fixes (user chose "fix now").** (1) A new incident's baseline checks (DNS for the host, HTTP for the target) run before the first AI turn (`pending-baseline`), and the rule path reuses the same helper. (2) `propose` moved before `narrate`, so the card shows while the narration streams. (3) `fillEmptyCause` puts the findings' cited cause back when the citation check empties "Likely cause:". Redeployed and re-timed live: first check card 22 s → 7.5 s; demo card 35 s → 9.6 s; both runs `done` in 1 AI turn; the cause is filled. 279 neurons for 2 runs. **Folded into TDD §8.2, §9.2.** | WP-13 live walkthrough: about 100 s against the 60 s target, with every run hitting the turn cap |
413	| 2026-10-15 19:15 | 13 | **First deploy.** D1 migrated remotely; the demo Worker went first, then `DEMO_BASE_URL` was set, then the main Worker (TDD §15). On the live site, PRD §13 steps 1–5 pass: workspace → cloudflare.com healthy (`dns-1`/`http-1` pass, resolved) → 500 errors → `origin_5xx` citing `[http-1]` with the card → Approve → verified → resolved (30 s). `169.254.169.254`, `localhost` and `10.0.0.1` were refused by the SSRF guard with clear messages, and no fetch was made. CPU: no event was cut off; details in TDD §15. 907 neurons for 5 runs. **Issues found:** (1) **slow** — the card came about 35 s after asking (≈ 5 s Workflow queue, 3 AI turns of 3–10 s, 13 s narration), so the full walkthrough is about 100 s against the PRD's 60 s; (2) the live model asks for **one check per turn**, so every run ends `limit_turns` and the UI shows a turn-limit note; (3) the live narration once left **"Likely cause:" empty** after the citation check removed an uncited sentence. | Found during the WP-13 live walkthrough |
414	| 2026-10-15 19:00 | 12 | **Live AI check (one 500-errors run, local, `LLM_MODE=live`).** The model proposed `restore_demo_service` **itself** (the demo hint works; the rule merge wasn't needed). Card at 23.5 s → Approve → verified → resolved at 53 s. Cost 199 neurons (estimate was about 100); the daily local total is 2,855. The run ended `limit_turns` with 3 turns and 1 check: most likely the model's `dns_lookup` of `localhost` was refused (it isn't a public hostname), which cost an extra turn. That's local only; recheck on the deployed `workers.dev` demo in WP-13. | User asked for the live check after WP-12 |
415	| 2026-10-15 18:58 | 12 | **Flaky `approval.test.ts` fixed.** It was a race in the test, not a timeout. The Workflow proposes the card one step before `finish`, so a poll could see the card while the run was still active, and the test's `activeRunId: null` check failed. The dedupe test could also have hit `run_active`. The tests now wait for "card shown and run finished" (`cardShown`), and the 20 s wait went back to 8 s. | Caught in 1 of 5 full runs (`approval.test.ts:51`). Polling every 1 ms reproduced it in 4 of 5 runs; with the fix, 8 of 8 passed |
416	| 2026-10-15 18:01 | 12 | **Act as built.** (1) **Rule merge** (user decision): `pickAction` adds `restore_demo_service` to the model's actions for any non-healthy category, and the planning prompt says when the target is the user's demo, so a broken demo always gets the card. (2) **Verify and monitor quick checks run inside the agent** (`afterAction`, `monitorTick`), not in the Workflow, so they don't use its 45-subrequest budget; `tailReserve` 4 → 8 for propose, outcome, action and verify. (3) The run finishes before the approval wait (D13), and the tail runs outside the main `try`. (4) Decisions are recorded in the agent first, then the Workflow is told; `expired` overrides an `approved` card that never ran; resolving by hand closes pending cards and stops the monitor. (5) The card says "verified" only after the re-check (`outcome.verified`). **Folded into TDD §6.3, §7.2, §7.6, §8.2–§8.4, §12.2, §14.** | WP-11's live runs showed the model leaving the action out. The browser walkthrough found the card saying "verified" before the re-check ran; fixed. Local walkthrough with the mock LLM: errors, slow, redirect_loop, bad_cache and slow + errors all went break → card → Approve → mitigated → resolved in 32–45 s; reject → investigating; a viewer sees no buttons |
417	| 2026-10-15 17:30 | 11 | **Combined demo modes** (user request): the demo holds a set of modes, either `["healthy"]` or one main problem (errors, redirect_loop, bad_cache) plus an optional `slow`. The RPC is now `getModes`/`setModes` and the API takes `{modes}` (400 `invalid_modes`). `restore_demo_service` will call `setModes(ws, ["healthy"])`. The panel is a "Problem" radio group plus an "Also slow" checkbox. The diagnosis still names one category; slow + bad_cache comes out as `high_latency` because latency is checked before caching. **Folded into TDD §7.1, §7.5, §7.6, §8.2, §14.** | The user chose "one problem + slow" and "main problem, slowness as evidence". The other problems clash: a page can't both 500 and redirect, and bad caching is invisible behind either |
418	| 2026-10-15 17:11 | 11 | **Local demo URL:** under `vite dev` the demo Worker has no port of its own, and a separate `wrangler dev` would keep its own mode state. So the main Worker passes `/w/*` through `env.DEMO.fetch()` only when `DEMO_BASE_URL` is its own origin (local dev), and `.dev.vars` points both `DEMO_BASE_URL` and `SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN` at `http://localhost:5173`. Deployed, `/w/*` on the main Worker is a 404. `DemoAdmin` throws plain `invalid_mode`/`invalid_workspace` (a ZodError loses its message over RPC). Below `lg` the panel is a closed disclosure instead of hidden. **Folded into TDD §4, §5.1, §7.1, §7.5, §12.1, §14.** | Spike: local Workflow probes reach `localhost:5173` despite `global_fetch_strictly_public`. A local run of all five modes gave the right category each time. Note: the local `.dev.vars` had `LLM_MODE=live`, so these 12 runs used the live model; the daily local estimate is 2,656 neurons |

import { cloudflareTest, readD1Migrations } from "@cloudflare/vitest-plugin";
import { defineConfig } from "vitest/config";

// Stand-in for the demo Worker behind the main Worker's DEMO binding. Modes live in memory, and every
// setModes is recorded so tests can assert on what the main Worker asked for (calls()). Its default fetch
// serves /w/<ws>/ by mode (500 when "errors" is on, otherwise 200), so the fake internet can route the test
// demo host here and approval tests see the restore take effect. The real demo is tested in "demo".
const demoStub = `
import { WorkerEntrypoint } from "cloudflare:workers";
const modes = new Map();
const calls = [];
export class DemoAdmin extends WorkerEntrypoint {
  ping() { return "pong"; }
  getModes(ws) { return modes.get(ws) ?? ["healthy"]; }
  setModes(ws, m) { calls.push({ ws, modes: m }); modes.set(ws, m); return m; }
  calls(ws) { return calls.filter((c) => c.ws === ws); }
  fetch(request) { return new Response("demo stub " + new URL(request.url).pathname); }
}
export default {
  fetch(request) {
    const [, w, ws] = new URL(request.url).pathname.split("/");
    if (w !== "w" || !ws) return new Response("demo stub");
    const broken = (modes.get(ws) ?? []).includes("errors");
    return new Response(broken ? "Internal Server Error" : "<html><body>demo ok</body></html>", { status: broken ? 500 : 200, headers: { "content-type": "text/html" } });
  },
};
`;

// The integration project's only "internet" (outboundService): DoH answers for *.example.com and the test
// demo host, so Workflow runs are offline and deterministic. nxdomain.example.com doesn't exist,
// broken.example.com returns 500, every other *.example.com host serves a small HTML page, and the test demo
// host is served by the demo stub (through its DEMO binding here).
const DEMO_HOST = "incident-copilot-demo.test.workers.dev";
const fakeInternet = `
const dns = (body) => new Response(JSON.stringify(body), { headers: { "content-type": "application/dns-json" } });
export default {
  async fetch(request, env) {
    const url = new URL(request.url);
    if (url.hostname === "cloudflare-dns.com" || url.hostname === "dns.google") {
      const name = (url.searchParams.get("name") || "").replace(/\\.$/, "");
      const known = name.endsWith("example.com") || name === "${DEMO_HOST}";
      if (!known || name === "nxdomain.example.com") return dns({ Status: 3 });
      const a = url.searchParams.get("type") === "A";
      return dns({ Status: 0, Answer: a ? [{ name, type: 1, TTL: 300, data: "93.184.215.14" }] : [] });
    }
    if (url.hostname === "${DEMO_HOST}") return env.DEMO.fetch(request);
    if (url.hostname === "broken.example.com") return new Response("upstream exploded", { status: 500 });
    if (url.hostname.endsWith("example.com")) return new Response("<html><body>ok</body></html>", { headers: { "content-type": "text/html" } });
    return new Response("fake internet: unknown host " + url.hostname, { status: 502 });
  },
};
`;

export default defineConfig({
  test: {
    projects: [
      {
        test: { name: "unit", include: ["test/unit/**/*.test.ts"], environment: "node" },
      },
      {
        plugins: [
          cloudflareTest(async () => ({
            wrangler: { configPath: "./wrangler.jsonc" },
            // The AI binding is remote by default: tests must not need a login or spend neurons.
            remoteBindings: false,
            miniflare: {
              bindings: {
                TEST_MIGRATIONS: await readD1Migrations("./migrations"),
                LLM_MODE: "mock",
                DEMO_BASE_URL: `https://${DEMO_HOST}`,
                // Runs start only when a test calls launchRun, so agent tests can drive runs by hand.
                DIAGNOSIS_AUTOSTART: "off",
              },
              outboundService: "fake-internet",
              workers: [
                { name: "incident-copilot-demo", modules: true, script: demoStub, compatibilityDate: "2026-10-01" },
                { name: "fake-internet", modules: true, script: fakeInternet, compatibilityDate: "2026-10-01", serviceBindings: { DEMO: "incident-copilot-demo" } },
              ],
            },
          })),
        ],
        test: {
          name: "integration",
          include: ["test/integration/**/*.test.ts"],
          setupFiles: ["./test/integration/setup.ts"],
        },
      },
      {
        plugins: [cloudflareTest({ wrangler: { configPath: "./demo-service/wrangler.jsonc" } })],
        test: { name: "demo", include: ["test/demo/**/*.test.ts"] },
      },
    ],
  },
});

774	- **Projects** (`vitest.config.ts`): `unit` runs in node. `integration` loads `wrangler.jsonc` with `LLM_MODE=mock` and a valid test `DEMO_BASE_URL`, and with `remoteBindings: false`, so the AI binding never goes remote: tests need no login and spend no neurons. `demo` loads `demo-service/wrangler.jsonc`.
775	- **Fake internet (WP-09):** the integration project's `outboundService` is an inline `fake-internet` Worker. It answers DoH for `*.example.com` (NXDOMAIN for `nxdomain.example.com`), returns 500 for `broken.example.com` and serves HTML for other `*.example.com` hosts, so no integration test touches the real network. `diagnosis.test.ts` runs real local Workflows through `introspectWorkflow`; `plan.test.ts` covers every limit with a fake `io`.

777	- **Demo stub:** the test runtime won't start while a service binding points at a Worker it doesn't know about. So `integration` adds a small inline stand-in `incident-copilot-demo` Worker behind `DEMO`. It keeps modes in memory, records every `setModes`, and exposes the record as `calls(ws)`. `demo-api.test.ts` and `approval.test.ts` assert what the main Worker asks it to do (e.g. `setModes`). The real demo service is tested only in `demo`.
778	- Tests use `env` and `exports` from `cloudflare:workers` (the plugin's `cloudflare:test` `env`/`SELF` are deprecated). Workflow tests use the plugin's Workflow introspection APIs. Retrying events with `waitForEvent` and the timeout path are verified in WP-02.

780	## 15. CI/CD and IaC
781	
782	- **`ci.yml`** (on PRs): `pnpm i --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test`, then `terraform fmt -check` and `terraform init -backend=false && terraform validate` (D15).
783	- **`deploy.yml`** (on push to `main`): runs the tests, `wrangler d1 migrations apply DB --remote` and `vite build`, deploys `demo-service`, then deploys main, then runs a smoke test.
784	  - Deploys use `cloudflare/wrangler-action`.
785	  - The smoke test calls `GET /api/health`, then `GET /api/session`, then `POST /api/demo/mode {modes:["healthy"]}`, then `POST /api/incidents "diagnose demo"`. It polls the snapshot until `activeRunId` is null and expects `category = healthy`. Run it with `LLM_MODE` live but only once per deploy, or skip the LLM with a `?smoke=rule` flag that forces `rule_based`.
786	- **Secrets:** `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` live in GitHub. `WEBHOOK_KEY` is set once with `wrangler secret put`.
787	- **First deploy (WP-13, by hand from `dev`):**
788	  1. `wrangler d1 migrations apply DB --remote`.
789	  2. `pnpm build`, which writes both Workers to `dist/`.
790	  3. **Demo first:** `wrangler deploy -c dist/incident_copilot_demo/wrangler.json`. The main Worker's `DEMO` binding needs the demo to exist.
791	  4. Set `DEMO_BASE_URL` in `wrangler.jsonc` to the deployed demo.
792	  5. `pnpm build`, then `wrangler deploy`. `.wrangler/deploy/config.json` points it at `dist/incident_copilot/wrangler.json`.
793	
794	  Results: the main Worker is about 485 KiB gzipped with a 53 ms start-up; the demo is 26 KiB. `.dev.vars` (and with it `SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN`) is never uploaded. The first request after the deploy got one transient `1042`; it didn't happen again.
795	- **CPU (R9), from `wrangler tail --format json` during the live walkthrough:** 310 events, none cut off (all `ok`, plus `canceled` for closed sockets and the paused approval wait).
796	  - The demo Worker peaked at 2 ms.
797	  - The main Worker's HTTP routes stayed ≤ 8 ms.
798	  - Inside the Durable Object and the Workflow, CPU went above 10 ms: `narrate` 182 ms (streaming), the Workflow `run` 49 ms, agent init and snapshot calls up to 41 ms, `afterAction` 21 ms, a monitor alarm 13 ms. Nothing failed, but `narrate` is the hottest path; watch it if Error 1102 ever shows up.
799	- **Terraform (`infra/`, local state, gitignored):** Cloudflare provider v5 with `cloudflare_d1_database`, plus the AI Gateway resource if the provider supports it. Otherwise the gateway is created in the dashboard and the README says so (R1). The `database_id` output is copied into `wrangler.jsonc` once. Wrangler owns everything else (§5.1).
800	
801	## 16. LLM eval (`eval/run.ts`)
802	
803	`pnpm eval --base <url>` works like this:
804	1. Get a session cookie.
805	2. For each case (`slow` → `high_latency`, `errors` → `origin_5xx`, `redirect_loop` → `redirect_loop`, `bad_cache` → `cache_misconfiguration`, `healthy` → `healthy`): set the mode, create an incident with "what's wrong with my demo service?", and poll the snapshot until it's done.
806	3. Score each case on three checks: ① the category is correct, ② at least one citation points to a decisive check that exists (`http-*`), ③ `restore_demo_service` is proposed (for healthy, it must **not** be proposed).
807	4. Write `eval/results.md` with a table and the model used. The target is at least 4 of 5 passing.
808	
809	A full run costs about 3k neurons, so don't run it on a review day.
810	
811	## 17. Build plan (2 days)
812	
813	The estimates are focused hours with heavy AI help. **Day 1 is heavy (about 14 h).** If it runs over, WP-12 and WP-13 move to the start of Day 2 ahead of all Should items. Commit after each WP, and export the session transcript into `prompts/` when the day ends.

340	---
341	
342	## 11. Quality and delivery
343	
344	### 11.1 Tests
345	- **Unit:** SSRF guard (IP range table tests), DNS/HTTP result parsers, failure classification rules, evidence-reference post-check, webhook allowlist and encryption.
346	- **Integration** (`@cloudflare/vitest-plugin`, the renamed `vitest-pool-workers`, real Workers runtime): IncidentAgent state transitions, D1 queries (due watches), DiagnosisWorkflow with a **mocked LLM** (scripted tool calls), approval → action → verify path, demo service modes.
347	
348	### 11.2 CI/CD (GitHub Actions)
349	- On PR: install, typecheck, lint, unit and integration tests.
350	- On merge to `main`: `terraform plan`/`apply` (infra), then `wrangler deploy` (code), then smoke test against the live URL.
351	- Secrets in GitHub Actions secrets (Cloudflare API token, account ID). Nothing committed.
352	
353	### 11.3 Infrastructure as Code
354	- **Terraform** (Cloudflare provider): D1 database, AI Gateway (verify provider support; if unsupported, managed in wrangler/dashboard and noted in the README), and the Workers custom domain if used.
355	- **Wrangler** (`wrangler.jsonc`): Worker code, bindings (including **Rate Limiting** bindings, which are defined only here), Durable Object migrations, Workflows, Cron Triggers, Static Assets, D1 migrations.
356	- The README explains the split: long-lived account resources in Terraform; frequently deployed code and bindings via wrangler.
357	
358	### 11.4 LLM eval suite
359	- A script breaks the demo service in each mode (`slow`, `errors`, `redirect_loop`, `bad_cache`), runs a diagnosis against the deployed or local stack, and scores:
360	  - the correct **category** (`high_latency`, `origin_5xx`, `redirect_loop`, `cache_misconfiguration`),
361	  - at least one **valid evidence reference** to the decisive check,
362	  - **proposes** `restore_demo_service`.
363	- Also a `healthy` control case (must not report a failure).
364	- Output: a pass-rate table committed as `eval/results.md`. **Target: ≥ 4/5 cases pass** (4 failure modes + healthy).
365	
366	---
367	
368	## 12. Milestones

370	Each milestone ends with a deployed, working app.

372	| Milestone | Scope | Priority | Est. |
373	|---|---|---|---|
374	| **M1 — Core loop** | Repo scaffold, Worker + Static Assets, workspace cookie, IncidentAgent, DiagnosisWorkflow with agent loop and bounds, `dns_lookup` + `http_probe`, SSRF guard, streaming chat with check cards, evidence references, deterministic fallback, AI Gateway routing, deploy | Must | 2.5 d |
375	| **M2 — Act** | Demo service + break panel, approval cards with `waitForEvent`, `restore_demo_service`, `add_to_watchlist`, verify after action, state machine, `cert_info` + `provider_status` | Must (tools: Should) | 1 d |
376	| **M3 — Watch** | D1 schema, watchlist UI, Cron Trigger, WatchWorkflow (confirm 2/3, auto-open, auto-resolve), unread badge, Slack/Discord webhooks, rate limits, budget guard | Should | 1 d |
377	| **M4 — Quality** | Unit + integration tests, GitHub Actions CI/CD, Terraform, eval suite + results | Must (tests + CI) / Should (Terraform, eval) | 1.5 d |

384	## 13. Success criteria and demo script
385	
386	### Success criteria
387	- All four assignment components are in use and explained in the README.
388	- A reviewer can complete the demo script below on the live URL in **≤ 60 seconds**, without reading docs.
389	- Eval suite ≥ 4/5; CI green; integration tests cover the approval path.
390	- Probing a private address such as `http://169.254.169.254` or `http://localhost` is refused with a clear message.
391	
392	### 60-second reviewer walkthrough
393	1. Open the live URL → the chat loads with a workspace already created.
394	2. Type `diagnose cloudflare.com` → check cards fill in → healthy diagnosis with evidence.
395	3. In **Demo service**, flip **500 errors** on.
396	4. Ask "what's wrong with my demo service?" → `origin_5xx` diagnosis citing `[http-1]` → approval card "Restore demo service".
397	5. Click **Approve** → re-check passes → incident `mitigated` → `resolved`.
398	6. Click **Postmortem** → markdown summary of what just happened.
399	7. (Optional) Hold the mic and ask by voice; add the demo to the watchlist and break it again to see an incident open itself.

403	## 14. Prompt-history plan (assignment requirement)
404	
405	- All build work happens in **dedicated Claude Code sessions inside the repo**, separate from other projects.
406	- `prompts/` contains the **full, unedited transcripts**, exported per session. The only edits are removed secrets (API keys, account IDs, tokens), and `PROMPTS.md` says so.
407	- `PROMPTS.md`: a short overview of how AI was used, then the most important prompts grouped by phase (planning, agent loop, Durable Objects/Workflows, safety, tests, debugging), each linking to its transcript.
408	- **This planning session (idea selection, the grilling interview and this PRD) is included as the "planning" phase.**
409	- Small, frequent commits, so git history lines up with the transcripts.

414	
415	| # | Item | Mitigation |
416	|---|---|---|
417	| R1 | Terraform provider may not support AI Gateway | Manage it in wrangler/dashboard; document in the README. |
418	| R2 | Agents SDK API changes | Pin the version; keep the Agent thin (state + WebSocket); business logic in plain modules. |
419	| R3 | Free allocation exhausted while reviewers try the app | Budget guard → deterministic mode with banner; optionally enable Workers Paid for the review window. |
420	| R4 | crt.sh rate limits, slowness or downtime | Short timeout, cache results 1 h, treat failure as "certificate info unavailable" (not a failure signal). |
421	| R5 | Probes come from one Cloudflare location | State this clearly in the UI and README; don't claim regional reachability. |
422	| R6 | gpt-oss-120b tool calling misbehaves | Schema validation, bounded loop, glm-4.7-flash fallback, deterministic fallback, eval suite to catch regressions. |
423	| R7 | Workflows step/payload size limits with large evidence | Store full evidence in the DO; pass only IDs and truncated summaries between steps. |
424	| R8 | Voice mis-transcription of hostnames | The transcript is always confirmed before sending; normalize "dot" → "." in post-processing. |
425	| R9 | Exceeding the Free 10 ms CPU limit (Error 1102) on a step or request | Byte-capped reads; bounded crt.sh parsing; no server-side rendering; check CPU time in Workers Logs during M1. |
426	| R10 | Exceeding 50 subrequests per Workflow run | ≤ 40 budget with a per-run counter (§6.2); redirect hops and retries counted. |
427	| R11 | Build-time testing uses up the AI allocation | Mock LLM in tests and `LLM_MODE=mock` for UI work; run the eval suite deliberately. |

# Session transcripts

Full transcripts of the Claude Code sessions that built Incident Copilot, exported per session (PRD §14). [`PROMPTS.md`](../PROMPTS.md) is the running log of every prompt; these files hold the whole conversations behind it.

## Day 1 (2026-10-15)

| [2026-10-15-0009-45c103a1.md](2026-10-15-0009-45c103a1.md) | 2026-10-15 00:09 → 00:16 | 2 | Task board from TDD.md |

| [2026-10-15-1335-0085bc8e.md](2026-10-15-1335-0085bc8e.md) | 2026-10-15 13:35 → 13:39 | 2 | cf_ai_incident_copilot pull request 2 review |

The last session was still running when it was exported (Day 1 wrap-up). Its remaining turns go into the Day 2 export.

## What each file contains

- Every user message, every reply from Claude, every tool call with its full input, and every tool result in full, in order and with timestamps.
- System reminders that the editor added to user messages are kept, folded under "system reminder".

## What was left out or changed

- **Secrets were removed and replaced with placeholders:**
  - `<ACCOUNT_ID>` for the Cloudflare account ID, including partial copies;
  - `<CLIENT_IP>` for the developer's IP address, which appeared in `wrangler tail` request logs;
  - `<TOKEN>`, `<API_KEY>`, `<GITHUB_TOKEN>` and `<SECRET>` for any token or key. None were found, but the export checks for them.
- **Not exported, because these aren't conversation text:**
  - screenshots and attached documents, which are binary;
  - the model's private reasoning blocks;
  - the editor's own bookkeeping entries (titles, file snapshots, queue events).

Nothing else was edited.

(eval):1: no matches found: --include=*.ts
----
import { Hono } from "hono";
import { incidentAgent } from "../agent/stub";
import type { AppEnv } from "../env";
import { isId, newId } from "../lib/ids";
import { parseTarget } from "../safety/target";
import { demoTarget } from "./session";

type IndexRow = { id: string; target: string; status: string; origin: string; unread: number; updated_at: number };

const MAX_MESSAGE = 2000;

/** Incident API (TDD §7.1). Every route is scoped to the caller's workspace cookie. */
export const incidents = new Hono<AppEnv>()
  .post("/", async (c) => {
    const body = await c.req.json<{ message?: unknown }>().catch(() => ({}) as { message?: unknown });
    const message = typeof body.message === "string" ? body.message.trim().slice(0, MAX_MESSAGE) : "";
    if (!message) return c.json({ error: "empty_message" }, 400);
    const workspaceId = c.get("workspaceId");
    const demoUrl = demoTarget(c.env.DEMO_BASE_URL, workspaceId);
    const target = parseTarget(message, demoUrl ?? "https://demo.invalid/");
    if (target.ok && target.source === "demo" && !demoUrl) return c.json({ error: "demo_unavailable" }, 503);
    if (!target.ok) return c.json({ error: target.error }, 400);
    // WP-17: run quota (D14) before init.
    const id = newId();
    const agent = await incidentAgent(c.env, id);
    await agent.init({ workspaceId, target: target.url, origin: "chat", message });
    return c.json({ id });
  })
  .get("/", async (c) => {
    const { results } = await c.env.DB.prepare(
      "SELECT id, target, status, origin, unread, updated_at FROM incidents_index WHERE workspace_id = ? ORDER BY updated_at DESC LIMIT 50",
    )
      .bind(c.get("workspaceId"))
      .all<IndexRow>();
    return c.json(results.map((r) => ({ id: r.id, target: r.target, status: r.status, origin: r.origin, unread: r.unread === 1, updatedAt: r.updated_at })));
  })
  .get("/:id", async (c) => {
    const id = c.req.param("id");
    if (!(await owns(c.env.DB, c.get("workspaceId"), id))) return c.json({ error: "not_found" }, 404);
    const agent = await incidentAgent(c.env, id);
    return c.json(await agent.getSnapshot());
  })
  .post("/:id/read", async (c) => {
    const id = c.req.param("id");
    if (!(await owns(c.env.DB, c.get("workspaceId"), id))) return c.json({ error: "not_found" }, 404);
    await (await incidentAgent(c.env, id)).markRead();
    return c.body(null, 204);
  });

async function owns(db: D1Database, workspaceId: string, id: string): Promise<boolean> {
  if (!isId(id)) return false;
  return !!(await db.prepare("SELECT 1 FROM incidents_index WHERE id = ? AND workspace_id = ?").bind(id, workspaceId).first());
}
import { Hono } from "hono";
import type { AppEnv } from "../env";
import { LIMITS } from "../lib/limits";

export function demoUrlFor(baseUrl: string, workspaceId: string): string {
  return `${baseUrl.replace(/\/+$/, "")}/w/${workspaceId}/`;
}

/** The demo URL, or null while `DEMO_BASE_URL` still holds the `<sub>` placeholder (set locally in WP-11, deployed in WP-13). */
export function demoTarget(baseUrl: string, workspaceId: string): string | null {
  const url = demoUrlFor(baseUrl, workspaceId);
  return URL.canParse(url) ? url : null;
}

export const session = new Hono<AppEnv>().get("/", (c) =>
  c.json({
    demoUrl: demoUrlFor(c.env.DEMO_BASE_URL, c.get("workspaceId")),
    // WP-17 wires this to the neuron budget guard (TDD §11).
    budgetMode: "ai" as "ai" | "rule_based",
    limits: LIMITS,
  }),
);

import type { AppEnv } from "../env";

export const health = new Hono<AppEnv>().get("/", (c) => c.json({ ok: true, version: c.env.CF_VERSION_METADATA.id }));

224	  addPendingChecks(runId: string, turn: number, calls: PlannedCall[]): string[] {
225	    this.mustRun(runId);

235	    });

  /** One planning run at a time (D13). The check and insert run without an await in between. */
  async startRun(question: string, kind: RunKind, role: "user" | "system" = "user"): Promise<string> {
    if (!this.incident()) throw new Error("not_initialized");
    if (this.activeRunId()) throw new Error("run_active");
    const seq = (this.sql<{ seq: number | null }>`SELECT MAX(seq) AS seq FROM runs`[0]?.seq ?? 0) + 1;
    const runId = `run-${seq}`;
    const now = Date.now();
    this.sql`INSERT INTO runs (id, seq, kind, question, status, started_at) VALUES (${runId}, ${seq}, ${kind}, ${question}, 'running', ${now})`;
    this.addMessage(runId, role, question);
    this.event("run_started", { runId, kind });
    this.touch();
    this.syncState();
    await this.upsertIndex();
    // Test seam: the integration tests drive some runs by hand (vitest.config.ts sets it); unset everywhere else.
    if ((this.env as { DIAGNOSIS_AUTOSTART?: string }).DIAGNOSIS_AUTOSTART !== "off") await this.launchRun(runId);
    return runId;
  }

import { getCookie, setCookie } from "hono/cookie";
import type { AppEnv } from "../env";
import { isId, newId } from "./ids";

/** Cookie name without its prefix; Hono adds `__Host-`, which forces Secure, Path=/ and no Domain. */
const WS_COOKIE = "ws";
const ONE_YEAR_S = 365 * 24 * 60 * 60;

export function readWorkspaceId(c: Context): string | undefined {
  const value = getCookie(c, WS_COOKIE, "host");
  return isId(value) ? value : undefined;
}

/** The workspace ID from a raw Cookie header: WebSocket upgrades reach the agent without Hono. */
export function workspaceFromCookieHeader(header: string | null): string | undefined {
  const value = /(?:^|;\s*)__Host-ws=([^;]*)/.exec(header ?? "")?.[1];
  return isId(value) ? value : undefined;
}

/**
 * Anonymous workspace identity (FR-8). A well-formed cookie is trusted without a D1 read; a forged ID
 * only creates a fresh, empty workspace. Missing or malformed cookies get a new workspace.
 */
export const workspace: MiddlewareHandler<AppEnv> = async (c, next) => {
  let id = readWorkspaceId(c);
  if (!id) {
    id = newId();
    await c.env.DB.prepare("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO workspaces (id, created_at) VALUES (?, ?)").bind(id, Date.now()).run();
    setCookie(c, WS_COOKIE, id, {
      prefix: "host",
      httpOnly: true,
      secure: true,
      sameSite: "Lax",
      path: "/",
      maxAge: ONE_YEAR_S,
    });
  }
  c.set("workspaceId", id);
  await next();
};
import { z } from "zod";

/**
 * JSON Schema for an LLM tool's parameters. Regex `pattern`s are left out: glm builds a constrained grammar
 * from the schema and rejects look-around ("3030: Grammar error", WP-07 live check). zod still validates
 * the arguments server-side, so nothing is lost.
 */
export function toolParameters(schema: z.ZodType): Record<string, unknown> {
  const json = z.toJSONSchema(schema, { io: "input" }) as Record<string, unknown>;
  delete json.$schema;
  return stripPatterns(json) as Record<string, unknown>;
}

function stripPatterns(node: unknown): unknown {
  if (Array.isArray(node)) return node.map(stripPatterns);
  if (node === null || typeof node !== "object") return node;
  // A property named "pattern" has an object value, so only the string keyword is dropped.
  return Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(node).flatMap(([k, v]) => (k === "pattern" && typeof v === "string" ? [] : [[k, stripPatterns(v)]])));
}
import type { ApprovalView } from "../../worker/agent/state";

export type CardState = { text: string; tone: "waiting" | "working" | "good" | "bad" | "closed" };

/** What an approval card says about itself (TDD §12.2): always in words, never colour alone. */
export function cardState(a: Pick<ApprovalView, "status" | "outcome">): CardState {
  switch (a.status) {
    case "pending":
      return { text: "Waiting for your approval", tone: "waiting" };
    case "approved":
      if (!a.outcome) return { text: "Approved, working on it…", tone: "working" };
      if (!a.outcome.ok) return a.outcome.verified === false ? { text: "Approved, but still failing", tone: "bad" } : { text: "Approved, but the action failed", tone: "bad" };
      // The action ran; the card only says "verified" once the re-check after it has passed.
      return a.outcome.verified ? { text: "Approved and verified", tone: "good" } : { text: "Approved, checking it…", tone: "working" };
    case "rejected":
      return { text: "Rejected", tone: "closed" };
    case "expired":
      return { text: "Expired, no action taken", tone: "closed" };
    case "superseded":
      return { text: "Replaced by a newer proposal", tone: "closed" };
  }
}

/** The "why" as markdown: the cause, with its evidence refs appended when the text doesn't cite them itself. */
export function whyText(a: Pick<ApprovalView, "cause" | "evidence">): string {
  if (/\[[a-z]+-\d+\]/.test(a.cause) || a.evidence.length === 0) return a.cause;
  return `${a.cause.replace(/\.?\s*$/, "")} ${a.evidence.map((id) => `[${id}]`).join(" ")}.`;
}
import type { IncidentStatus, RunStatus, TimelineView } from "../../worker/agent/state";
import { parseTarget } from "../../worker/safety/target";

/**
 * A follow-up that names a different host than the incident's (TDD §8.6). Tools are pinned to the incident's
 * origin (D6), so the UI offers a new incident instead of sending. "demo" alone doesn't count.
 */
export function otherHost(text: string, incidentHost: string): string | null {
  const r = parseTarget(text, "https://demo.invalid/");
  if (!r.ok || r.source === "demo") return null;
  return r.host === incidentHost.toLowerCase() ? null : r.host;
}

const LIMIT_NOTES: Partial<Record<RunStatus, string>> = {
  limit_turns: "Stopped at the 3-turn limit.",
  limit_tools: "Stopped at the 6-check limit.",
  limit_budget: "Stopped when this run's request budget ran out.",
  limit_deadline: "Stopped at the 60-second limit.",
};

/**
 * Says which limit ended the latest run (TDD §8.2 "Limits keep progress"), or that it failed. Nothing while
 * a newer run is in progress, or when the run simply finished.
 */
export function runNote(timeline: readonly TimelineView[]): string | null {
  const last = [...timeline].reverse().find((t) => t.type === "run_finished" || t.type === "run_started");
  if (!last || last.type !== "run_finished") return null;
  const status = last.detail.status as RunStatus;
  if (status === "failed") return "This run failed before it finished. Try asking again.";
  const note = LIMIT_NOTES[status];
  return note ? `${note} The diagnosis uses the checks it completed.` : null;
}

export const STATUS_TEXT: Record<IncidentStatus, string> = {
  investigating: "Investigating",
  awaiting_approval: "Awaiting approval",
  mitigated: "Mitigated",
  resolved: "Resolved",
};

## 1. Decisions log

These are binding for the build. "Dev" marks a deliberate deviation from the PRD.

| # | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Full PRD scope, built in strict Must→Should order with a cut line (§17). Anything not built is documented in the README as designed but not built. | 2-day window |
| D2 | Rate limits, quotas and the budget guard ship on **Day 2** (M3), as the PRD orders them. *Accepted risk:* the `workers.dev` URL is unprotected for about a day. Mitigation: don't share the URL until WP-17 lands. | The user chose to keep PRD ordering |
| D3 | `DiagnosisWorkflow` extends the Agents SDK's **`AgentWorkflow`** (`agents@0.28.1`, pinned exactly). It uses `runWorkflow`, `waitForApproval` and `approveWorkflow`/`rejectWorkflow`. `WatchWorkflow` and `NotifyWorkflow` are plain `WorkflowEntrypoint`s because cron or the Agent starts them without needing a callback. | Removes glue code. Logic stays in plain modules (PRD R2). |
| D4 | **Streaming:** the final Workflow step `step.do("narrate")` calls the Agent over RPC (`narrate(runId)`). The Agent calls the LLM with `stream: true`, sends deltas over the WebSocket, and stores the final text. It's idempotent per `runId` and counts as LLM turn 4. | Real streaming that stays durable (FR-3) |
| D5 | The **structured diagnosis comes from a `submit_findings` tool call** that ends planning: `{category, confidence, evidence[], actions[]}`, validated with zod. Narration only writes prose from those fixed findings. If the call is missing or invalid, the rule-based classifier fills the fields. | The structure is validated before any text streams, and the eval is deterministic |
| D6 | **Tools are pinned to the incident's origin.** `http_probe` accepts any path on the incident host. `dns_lookup` and `cert_info` accept the host or CNAME targets found in this incident's DNS evidence. `provider_status` uses a fixed list. A different host needs a new incident, and the UI offers a button. | Prevents use as an open proxy or a prompt-injection pivot |
| D7 | The demo is served at **`/w/<workspaceId>/`** as the PRD specifies. *Accepted risk:* the workspace ID is the session credential and appears in a public URL, prompts and logs. It's documented in the README security section. | The user chose to keep the PRD design |
| D8 | **Incident links are view-only for non-owners.** Connections are tagged `owner` or `viewer` from the cookie in `onConnect`. Every mutating callable requires `owner`. | Stops quota theft and approvals from someone who only has a link |
| D9 | **A deterministic target parser** reads the first message. It takes a URL first, then a hostname token, then the keyword "demo" (which maps to the demo URL). Otherwise it returns a 400 asking for a host. No LLM is involved, and the UI has a "Use demo service" chip. | Predictable, free, and keeps the first check card under 2 s |
| D10 | **Asset sub-probe:** when `http_probe` gets an HTML response, it also fetches the first same-origin script or stylesheet once and records the asset's cache headers in the same evidence. | Makes `cache_misconfiguration` detectable by the rules and the eval |
| D11 | Dev: **post-diagnosis monitoring runs on Agent scheduler ticks** (`this.schedule`), not inside Workflows. That covers the 15 s ticks after an action, the 60 s ticks for `recheck_burst`, and watch cron results feeding the same counter. Workflows end after verify (Diagnosis) or notify (Watch). | The 50-subrequest limit per Workflow run on Free |
| D12 | **Cron checks due watches inline**, with `LIMIT 6` per tick and a subrequest counter. `WatchWorkflow` starts only on failure. Dev: a **global cap of 30 active watches** across the app. | Fits the 50 subrequests per invocation (6 watches × up to 7 subrequests each) |
| D13 | **One planning run and one pending approval per incident.** The input is disabled while a run is active, and the server returns 409 for a second send. Follow-ups are allowed while an approval is pending. A different new proposal **supersedes** the pending one (`rejectWorkflow(old, "superseded")`). The same proposal is deduplicated. Approve and Reject are idempotent. | No conflicting actions and no piling up of cards |
| D14 | **A single "LLM run" quota.** New incidents, follow-ups and postmortems all count against 3/min (Rate Limiting binding) and 10/hour (D1). Voice has its own limit. Hitting a limit shows the time until reset. | Directly protects neurons |
| D15 | Dev: **Terraform uses local state.** CI only runs `fmt -check` and `validate`, and `apply` is run by hand from a laptop. CI does run D1 migrations and `wrangler deploy` on `main`. | Infrastructure is created once. No extra account needed. |
| D16 | **The sidebar polls `GET /api/incidents` every 10 s** while the tab is visible. New unread rows trigger a badge and a browser notification. | No extra DO, and the D1 index already exists |
| D17 | **UI uses Kumo** (`@cloudflare/kumo`, Cloudflare's design system as used on cloudflare.com). Fonts are open-licensed look-alikes, with Cloudflare's commercial fonts named first in the stack (§13.1). | The site's fonts are commercially licensed |
| D18 | Dev: **webhooks fire only for watch-originated incidents** (opened, mitigated, resolved). On-demand incidents have the user present, so the in-app channel covers them. Sending happens in `NotifyWorkflow`, whose instance ID `notify-<incidentId>-<state>` makes it at most once per state change. | FR-54 dedupe comes from the Workflow ID being unique |

## 11. Safety implementation

- **SSRF (`safety/ssrf.ts`, `iprange.ts`):**
  - Only `http` and `https` schemes, and only port 80/443 (an empty port is allowed).
  - Reject IP literals, `localhost`/`*.localhost`/`.local`/`.internal`, single-label hosts and URLs with credentials.
  - Resolve the host with DoH before fetching and reject it if **any** A or AAAA answer is in a blocked range: 0/8, 10/8, 100.64/10, 127/8, 169.254/16, 172.16/12, 192.0.0/24, 192.0.2/24, 192.168/16, 198.18/15, 198.51.100/24, 203.0.113/24, 224/4, 240/4, ::/128, ::1, fc00::/7, fe80::/10, ff00::/8, ::ffff:0:0/96 (checked against the embedded v4 address), 64:ff9b::/96, 2001:db8::/32. WP-04 also blocks ::/96 (IPv4-compatible), 100::/64, and 6to4 `2002::/16` checked against its embedded v4 address. Anything that doesn't parse is blocked.
  - Apply the same check to every redirect hop.
  - **Dev-only exception:** when `SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN` is set, that one exact origin (scheme, host and port, for example the local demo service) skips the checks above. It's set only in `.dev.vars` for local work and never in `wrangler.jsonc` `vars`, so in production the demo is reached through its public `workers.dev` URL and everything private stays blocked. There's no wildcard and no partial match.
  - Defense in depth: `global_fetch_strictly_public`. A DNS-rebinding race can't be fully closed, and the README says so.
- **Open proxy:** GET/HEAD only. No headers, bodies or cookies from the user. `reader.ts` caps reads at 16 KB, aborts after 10 s, and never follows redirects automatically. Origin pin (D6).
- **Probe rate:** `PROBE_LIMIT.limit({key: hostname})` runs before every probe sample. If it's exceeded, the probe returns `unavailable: rate_limited`.
- **Quotas (D14):** `RUN_LIMIT.limit({key: ws})` plus the D1 fixed-window `usage_counters(kind='run')`, which uses an atomic `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET count = count + 1 RETURNING count`.
- **Budget guard:** before every run, `llm_usage(today).neurons_est ≥ 9000` → `mode = rule_based`. `GET /api/session` returns `budgetMode`, which drives the `BudgetBanner`.
- **Webhooks:**
  - Only https URLs on `hooks.slack.com`, or `discord.com`/`discordapp.com` with a path starting `/api/webhooks/`.
  - Encrypted with AES-GCM-256 using the `WEBHOOK_KEY` secret, a 12-byte random IV, and AAD = workspaceId.
  - `url_hint` is stored masked (`hooks.slack.com/…/••••abcd`).
  - The URL is never sent to the LLM or written to logs.
  - Payload: `{text}` for Slack, `{content}` for Discord. It contains the summary, status and incident link.
- **Prompt injection:** `<untrusted>` wrapping, snippet truncation, zod plus origin-pin validation, the fixed action catalog, and human approval for every action.
- **Accepted risks:** D2 (unprotected for about a day), D7 (workspace ID in the demo URL), probes from a single location (R5).

## 3. Assignment requirement mapping

| # | Requirement | Implementation | Why this choice |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **LLM** | Workers AI `@cf/openai/gpt-oss-120b` (primary), `@cf/zai-org/glm-4.7-flash` (fallback), routed through **AI Gateway** | Strong tool calling and reasoning within the free allocation. The gateway adds fallback, logs, caching and rate limits. |
| 2 | **Workflow / coordination** | **Workflows**: `DiagnosisWorkflow` (agent loop, approvals) and `WatchWorkflow` (confirm → open → diagnose → notify) | Each LLM call and each check is a durable, retryable step. `waitForEvent` provides human approval with a timeout. |
| 3 | **User input via chat or voice** | React chat UI served by **Workers Static Assets** (Cloudflare's recommended successor to Pages), plus push-to-talk voice through Workers AI `whisper-large-v3-turbo` | One deploy and one URL. The WebSocket connects directly to the incident's Durable Object. |
| 4 | **Memory or state** | **Durable Objects** (Agents SDK `Agent`, one per incident, SQLite storage) and **D1** (incident index, watchlist job table, webhooks) | One actor per incident gives isolation and live updates. D1 is good at "which watches are due now?" queries. |

**Extra Cloudflare products:** AI Gateway, Cron Triggers, D1, Rate Limiting binding, Workers Static Assets, Workers AI speech-to-text, Service Bindings (demo service).

## Context

What exists today: 495 tests across `unit`, `integration` and `demo`; the approval path is already covered (`test/integration/approval.test.ts`, 8 cases). The only workflows are the Sift ones. There's no README, no `scripts/` folder, no `infra/` folder (Terraform is WP-25, below the cut line), and no way yet to force a rule-based run (`getRunContext` always returns `mode: "ai"`, see `worker/agent/IncidentAgent.ts:215`).

---

## WP-14 Tests (fill the gaps)

## WP-15 CI/CD

### Smoke rule mode (`?smoke=rule`, TDD §15)
- `worker/api/incidents.ts` POST: when `c.req.query("smoke") === "rule"`, pass `mode: "rule_based"` into `agent.init`.
- `worker/agent/IncidentAgent.ts`: add `mode?: RunMode` to `InitParams`. `init` → `startRun(question, kind, role, mode = "ai")` writes `runs.mode` (the column already exists in `schema.sql.ts`). `getRunContext` returns `run.mode` instead of the hardcoded `"ai"`, and the comment changes to say WP-17's budget guard also sets it.
- `loop/plan.ts` already skips the LLM when `ctx.mode === "rule_based"`, and `narrate` already uses the template for rule-based runs (`IncidentAgent.ts:290`), so a smoke run uses **0 neurons**.
- The public flag only makes a run cheaper; the README says so.
- Tests: `incidents.test.ts`: `?smoke=rule` creates a `rule_based` run, while no flag (or any other value) stays `ai`. `diagnosis.test.ts`: a smoke run on the healthy demo ends `healthy`, mode `rule_based`, with 0 LLM turns.

### `scripts/smoke.ts` (Node 22 runs `.ts` directly; single file, no imports)
`node scripts/smoke.ts <baseUrl>`:
1. `GET /api/health` with up to 5 retries (WP-13 saw one transient `1042` right after a deploy).
2. `GET /api/session` → take the `__Host-ws` cookie from `set-cookie` and send it on every later call.
3. `POST /api/demo/mode {modes:["healthy"]}`.
4. `POST /api/incidents?smoke=rule {message:"diagnose demo"}`.
5. Poll `GET /api/incidents/:id` every 2 s for up to 90 s until `activeRunId === null`.
6. Expect `category === "healthy"`. Print one line per step and exit 1 on any failure.

Add `scripts/**/*.ts` to `tsconfig.node.json` `include` and to the ESLint node-globals block, so the script is type-checked and linted.

### `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
- Runs on `pull_request` to `main`, plus `workflow_call` so the deploy workflow can reuse it. Permissions: `contents: read`. Concurrency per ref, cancelling older runs.
- Steps: checkout → `pnpm/action-setup@v4` (reads `packageManager`) → `setup-node@v4` (Node 22, pnpm cache) → `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` → `pnpm typecheck` → `pnpm lint` → `pnpm test`.
- Terraform steps (`setup-terraform`, `fmt -check`, `init -backend=false`, `validate`) run only when `hashFiles('infra/**/*.tf') != ''`, so they turn on by themselves if WP-25 is built.

### `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`
- Runs on `push` to `main`, plus `workflow_dispatch`. Concurrency group `deploy-production`, never cancelled.
- Job `ci` reuses `ci.yml`. Then job `deploy` (needs `ci`) uses the `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` secrets:
  1. install
  2. `wrangler d1 migrations apply DB --remote`
  3. `pnpm build`
  4. deploy the demo: `wrangler deploy -c dist/incident_copilot_demo/wrangler.json`
  5. deploy main: `wrangler deploy`, which uses `.wrangler/deploy/config.json` as in TDD §15
  6. `node scripts/smoke.ts ${{ steps.main.outputs.deployment-url }}`
- Steps 2, 4 and 5 use `cloudflare/wrangler-action@v3` with `packageManager: pnpm`, so the repo's pinned wrangler runs and the deployed URL comes from `deployment-url`. No URL is committed (D2).

### GitHub secrets (a step for you)
- Create an API token: the "Edit Cloudflare Workers" template plus **Account › D1 › Edit**.
- Run `gh secret set CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` yourself (it prompts for the value, so it never goes through me).
- With your OK at that step, I set `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` from `wrangler whoami` using `gh secret set`.

**Verify locally:** run `pnpm dev`, then `node scripts/smoke.ts http://localhost:5173` passes. Then push `dev`: PR #3 (`dev` → `main`) picks up `ci.yml` and should go green. "Merging deploys and passes smoke" is confirmed after **you** merge (I don't merge, per the branch rule). Until then, WP-15 stays 🔄 with a note, or ✅ with "deploy check pending merge"; I'll record whichever is true.

Fold-ins: TDD §14 (new tests) and §15 (smoke mode as built, Node 22 script, Terraform step gated on `infra/`, `deployment-url`), plus a TASKS decisions row. Commit `WP-15: …`.

## WP-16 README + PROMPTS

### `README.md` (new; written for reviewers of the assignment)
1. What it is, in one paragraph, plus the 60-second walkthrough (PRD §13). **No live URL until WP-17 is ✅ (D2)**; there's a placeholder line saying so.
2. How it meets the four assignment parts (PRD §3 table): LLM (Workers AI gpt-oss-120b → glm-4.7-flash via AI Gateway), coordination (Workflows + Durable Objects), chat input (voice only if WP-23 is built), memory/state (DO SQLite + D1).
3. Architecture: a mermaid diagram (browser ↔ Worker/Hono ↔ IncidentAgent DO ↔ DiagnosisWorkflow ↔ tools/SSRF guard ↔ internet/demo Worker; D1; AI Gateway), plus the request flow in short steps.
4. Agents SDK: `Agent` per incident (synced state, owner/viewer roles, callables, scheduler for monitor ticks), `AgentWorkflow` with `runWorkflow`/`waitForApproval`, and why the logic sits in plain modules (`loop/plan.ts`).
5. Agent loop and bounds: 3 turns, 6 tools, 60 s, 45 subrequests; `submit_findings`; citation check; "Limits keep progress"; rule-based fallback.
6. Security: SSRF guard, origin pin, read caps, GET/HEAD only, untrusted wrapping, human approval for every action, viewer links. **Accepted risks:** D2, D7, single probe location (R5), DNS rebinding. Also the `?smoke=rule` note.
7. Running locally: `pnpm i`, copy `.dev.vars.example` → `.dev.vars` (`LLM_MODE=mock` needs no account), `pnpm dev`, `pnpm test`. Deploying: first deploy by hand (TDD §15 order), then CI/CD and secrets.
8. Terraform / wrangler split: what each owns, and that today D1 was made with `wrangler d1 create` and the AI Gateway in the dashboard (R1, D15).
9. Tests and CI: three Vitest projects, fake internet, mock LLM, what CI and deploy do.
10. **Designed, not built:** WP-17 to WP-26, one line each from TDD. A new TASKS rule says to move an item out of this list when its WP lands, so the README stays true for the rest of Day 2.
11. AI usage → link to `PROMPTS.md`.

import type { IncidentStatus } from "./state";

/** Legal status edges (TDD §10). Every non-resolved status can be resolved by hand (FR-7). */
const EDGES: Record<IncidentStatus, readonly IncidentStatus[]> = {
  investigating: ["awaiting_approval", "mitigated", "resolved"],
  awaiting_approval: ["investigating", "mitigated", "resolved"],
  mitigated: ["investigating", "resolved"],
  resolved: [],
};

export class IllegalTransition extends Error {
  override name = "IllegalTransition";
}

export function canTransition(from: IncidentStatus, to: IncidentStatus): boolean {
  return EDGES[from].includes(to);
}

export function assertTransition(from: IncidentStatus, to: IncidentStatus): void {
  if (!canTransition(from, to)) throw new IllegalTransition(`Illegal transition ${from} → ${to}`);
}
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import type { IncidentStatus } from "../../worker/agent/state";
import { assertTransition, canTransition, IllegalTransition } from "../../worker/agent/transitions";

const ALL: IncidentStatus[] = ["investigating", "awaiting_approval", "mitigated", "resolved"];

/** TDD §10, written out edge by edge. */
const LEGAL: [IncidentStatus, IncidentStatus][] = [
  ["investigating", "awaiting_approval"],
  ["investigating", "resolved"],
  ["investigating", "mitigated"],
  ["awaiting_approval", "investigating"],
  ["awaiting_approval", "mitigated"],
  ["awaiting_approval", "resolved"],
  ["mitigated", "resolved"],
  ["mitigated", "investigating"],
];

describe("transitions", () => {
  it.each(LEGAL)("%s → %s is legal", (from, to) => {
    expect(canTransition(from, to)).toBe(true);
    expect(() => assertTransition(from, to)).not.toThrow();
  });

  const illegal = ALL.flatMap((from) => ALL.map((to) => [from, to] as [IncidentStatus, IncidentStatus])).filter(
    ([f, t]) => !LEGAL.some(([a, b]) => a === f && b === t),
  );

  it.each(illegal)("%s → %s throws", (from, to) => {
    expect(canTransition(from, to)).toBe(false);
    expect(() => assertTransition(from, to)).toThrow(IllegalTransition);
  });

  it("resolved is final", () => {
    expect(ALL.some((to) => canTransition("resolved", to))).toBe(false);
  });
});

function fakeMedia(matches: boolean) {
  const listeners = new Set<(e: MediaQueryListEvent) => void>();
  return {
    matches,
    addEventListener: (_: string, fn: (e: MediaQueryListEvent) => void) => listeners.add(fn),
    removeEventListener: (_: string, fn: (e: MediaQueryListEvent) => void) => listeners.delete(fn),
    fire: (dark: boolean) => listeners.forEach((fn) => fn({ matches: dark } as MediaQueryListEvent)),
    listeners,
  };
}

function fakeRoot() {
  return { dataset: {} as DOMStringMap } as HTMLElement;
}

describe("initTheme", () => {
  it("sets data-mode from the current preference", () => {
    const root = fakeRoot();
    initTheme(root, fakeMedia(true) as unknown as MediaQueryList);
    expect(root.dataset.mode).toBe("dark");
  });

  it("follows preference changes until cleaned up", () => {
    const root = fakeRoot();
    const media = fakeMedia(false);
    const stop = initTheme(root, media as unknown as MediaQueryList);

const ID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{32}$/;

/** 128-bit random hex ID for incidents, workspaces and approvals (TDD §6.4). */
export function newId(): string {
  const bytes = crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(16));
  return Array.from(bytes, (b) => b.toString(16).padStart(2, "0")).join("");
}

export function isId(value: unknown): value is string {
  return typeof value === "string" && ID_RE.test(value);
}

## 10. State machine (`agent/transitions.ts`)

| From | To | Trigger |
|---|---|---|
| (new) | `investigating` | `init` (chat message or confirmed watch failure) |
| `investigating` | `awaiting_approval` | `proposeAction` created a card |
| `investigating` | `resolved` | Findings `healthy` on the first run of a chat incident, or a manual resolve |
| `awaiting_approval` | `investigating` | Rejected or expired |
| `awaiting_approval` | `mitigated` | `afterAction` with a verify pass |
| `awaiting_approval` | `investigating` | `afterAction` with a verify fail |
| `investigating` | `mitigated` | A monitor or watch pass without an action |
| `mitigated` | `resolved` | 3 passes in a row |
| `mitigated` | `investigating` | A failure comes back |
| any non-resolved | `resolved` | Manual `resolve()` (FR-7) |

`transition(to, reason)` throws on an illegal edge. Every transition writes a timeline row, a `setState` update, an index upsert, and a NotifyWorkflow for watch incidents.

/** A fresh workspace: its cookie header for later requests. */
async function workspace(): Promise<string> {
  const res = await exports.default.fetch(`${BASE}/api/session`);
  const cookie = res.headers.getSetCookie().find((c) => c.startsWith("__Host-ws="))!;
  return cookie.split(";")[0]!;
}

    method: "POST",

    ...(body === undefined ? {} : { body: JSON.stringify(body) }),
  });

Checking my stub wording matches the TDD's watch design.

<details><summary>Tool result</summary>

```
### 8.5 Watch (cron, D12)

1. `scheduled()` runs `SELECT * FROM watches WHERE next_run <= now AND expires_at > now ORDER BY next_run LIMIT 6`.
2. For each watch, it runs `quickCheck` with a subrequest counter (stopping before 50) and updates `next_run`, `last_status` and `consecutive_passes`.
3. If `open_incident_id` is set, it calls `agent.reportWatchCheck(pass)`.
4. If the check fails and no incident is open, it calls `WATCH_WORKFLOW.create({id: "watch-<id>-<minute>"})`. Duplicate IDs are ignored.
5. Expired watches are deleted.

`WatchWorkflow`:
1. `step.sleep("30 seconds")`, then a re-check, done twice (2 of 3 failures confirm).
2. If not confirmed, end.
3. Otherwise `step.do("open")` creates an incident ID, calls `agent.init({origin: "watch", watchId})` and sets `watches.open_incident_id`. `init` starts the diagnosis.
4. `step.do("notify")` creates `NOTIFY_WORKFLOW` with `notify-<id>-opened`.

The Agent creates a NotifyWorkflow for watch incidents when they transition to mitigated or resolved (D18). When an incident resolves, the watch's `open_incident_id` is cleared.
```

</details>

  async setFindings(runId: string, findings: Findings, mode: RunMode = "ai"): Promise<void> {
    const run = this.mustRun(runId);
    const incident = this.mustIncident();
    this.sql`UPDATE runs SET findings_json = ${JSON.stringify(findings)}, mode = ${mode} WHERE id = ${runId}`;
    this.sql`UPDATE incident SET category = ${findings.category}, confidence = ${findings.confidence}, mode = ${mode} WHERE id = ${incident.id}`;
    this.event("findings", { runId, category: findings.category, confidence: findings.confidence, mode });
    // A chat incident whose first check is healthy is resolved right away (TDD §10).
    if (findings.category === "healthy" && run.seq === 1 && incident.origin === "chat" && incident.status === "investigating") {
      await this.transition("resolved", "healthy on first check");
      return;
    }
    this.touch();
    this.syncState();

import { introspectWorkflow } from "cloudflare:test";
import { env } from "cloudflare:workers";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { incidentAgent } from "../../worker/agent/stub";
import { newId } from "../../worker/lib/ids";

/**
 * Real DiagnosisWorkflow runs: local Workflows, the mock LLM (LLM_MODE=mock) and the fake internet from
 * vitest.config.ts (broken.example.com → 500, ok.example.com → 200, DoH for *.example.com).
 */
async function diagnoseRun(message: string, target: string) {
  const introspector = await introspectWorkflow(env.DIAGNOSIS_WORKFLOW);
  try {
    const id = newId();
    const agent = await incidentAgent(env, id);
    const { runId } = await agent.init({ workspaceId: newId(), target, origin: "chat", message });
    expect(await agent.launchRun(runId!)).toBe(`diag-${id}-1`);
    const [instance] = await introspector.get();
    await instance!.waitForStatus("complete");
    return { id, agent, runId: runId!, output: await instance!.getOutput(), snapshot: await agent.getSnapshot() };
  } finally {
    await introspector.dispose();
  }
}

const finished = (s: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof diagnoseRun>>["snapshot"]) => s.timeline.filter((t) => t.type === "run_finished").map((t) => t.detail.status);

describe("DiagnosisWorkflow (mock LLM, fake internet)", () => {
  it("diagnoses a failing origin end to end", async () => {
    const { snapshot: s, output, runId } = await diagnoseRun("why is broken.example.com down?", "https://broken.example.com/");
    // The baseline DNS + HTTP checks run before the first AI turn (WP-13b), so one turn is enough.
    expect(output).toMatchObject({ status: "done", mode: "ai", toolCalls: 2, llmTurns: 1, category: "origin_5xx" });
    expect(s.checks.map((c) => [c.evidenceId, c.status])).toEqual([
      ["dns-1", "pass"],
      ["http-1", "fail"],
    ]);
    expect(s).toMatchObject({ category: "origin_5xx", status: "investigating", activeRunId: null, mode: "ai" });
    const narration = s.messages.find((m) => m.id === `narr-${runId}`)!;
    expect(narration.content).toContain("[http-1]");
    expect(finished(s)).toEqual(["done"]);
  });

  it("resolves a healthy site on its first run", async () => {
    const { snapshot: s } = await diagnoseRun("is ok.example.com up?", "https://ok.example.com/");
    expect(s).toMatchObject({ category: "healthy", status: "resolved", activeRunId: null });
  });

  it("falls back to the rules when the LLM is down", async () => {
    const { snapshot: s, output } = await diagnoseRun("broken.example.com #llm-down", "https://broken.example.com/");
    expect(output).toMatchObject({ status: "llm_unavailable", mode: "rule_based", llmTurns: 1 });
  it("creates an incident for the demo and lists it for its workspace only", async () => {
    const mine = await workspace();
    const res = await post("/api/incidents", mine, { message: "what's wrong with my demo?" });
    expect(res.status).toBe(200);
    const { id } = await res.json<{ id: string }>();
    expect(id).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{32}$/);

    const list = await (await get("/api/incidents", mine)).json<{ id: string; status: string; target: string; unread: boolean }[]>();
    expect(list).toEqual([expect.objectContaining({ id, status: "investigating", origin: "chat", unread: false })]);
    expect(list[0]!.target).toMatch(/\/w\/[0-9a-f]{32}\/$/);
    expect(await (await get("/api/incidents", await workspace())).json()).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("serves the snapshot to the owner only", async () => {
    const mine = await workspace();
    const { id } = await (await post("/api/incidents", mine, { message: "diagnose shop.example.com" })).json<{ id: string }>();

    const snap = await get(`/api/incidents/${id}`, mine);
    expect(snap.status).toBe(200);
    expect(await snap.json()).toMatchObject({ id, target: "https://shop.example.com/", activeRunId: "run-1" });

    expect((await get(`/api/incidents/${id}`, await workspace())).status).toBe(404);
    expect((await get("/api/incidents/not-an-id", mine)).status).toBe(404);
  });

  it("marks an incident read", async () => {
    const mine = await workspace();
    const { id } = await (await post("/api/incidents", mine, { message: "diagnose shop.example.com" })).json<{ id: string }>();
    expect((await post(`/api/incidents/${id}/read`, mine)).status).toBe(204);
    expect((await post(`/api/incidents/${id}/read`, await workspace())).status).toBe(404);
  });
});

import { Hono } from "hono";
import { DemoModesSchema } from "../../demo-service/src/modes";
import type { AppEnv } from "../env";
import { demoTarget } from "./session";

/** The caller's demo service (TDD §7.1, §7.5). Modes change only through the DEMO binding, never over HTTP to the demo. */
export const demo = new Hono<AppEnv>()
  .get("/", async (c) => {
    const ws = c.get("workspaceId");
    const url = demoTarget(c.env.DEMO_BASE_URL, ws);
    if (!url) return c.json({ error: "demo_unavailable" }, 503);
    return c.json({ modes: await c.env.DEMO.getModes(ws), url });
  })
  .post("/mode", async (c) => {
    const ws = c.get("workspaceId");
    const url = demoTarget(c.env.DEMO_BASE_URL, ws);
    if (!url) return c.json({ error: "demo_unavailable" }, 503);
    const body = await c.req.json<{ modes?: unknown }>().catch(() => ({}) as { modes?: unknown });
    const parsed = DemoModesSchema.safeParse(body.modes);
    if (!parsed.success) return c.json({ error: "invalid_modes" }, 400);
    return c.json({ modes: await c.env.DEMO.setModes(ws, parsed.data), url });
  });
12:api.route("/health", health);
15:api.route("/session", session);
16:api.route("/incidents", incidents);
17:api.route("/demo", demo);

{ cookie } : {}), ...(body === undefined ? {} : { \"content-type\": \"application/json\" }) },\n    ...(body === undefined ? {} : { body: JSON.stringify(body) }),\n    redirect: \"manual\",\n  });\n  const ws = res.headers.getSetCookie().find((c) => c.startsWith(\"__Host-ws=\"));\n  if (ws) cookie = ws.split(\";\")[0]!;\n  const text = await res.text();\n  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`${method} ${path} → ${res.status} ${text.slice(0, 200)}`);\n  return JSON.parse(text) as T;\n}\n\nfunction step(name: string, detail: string): void {\n  console.log(`✓ ${name.padEnd(9)} ${detail}`);\n}\n\nasync function main(): Promise<void> {\n  // The first request after a deploy can fail once (WP-13 saw a transient 1042), so health retries.\n  for (let i = 1; ; i++) {\n    try {\n      const h = await call<{ ok: boolean; version?: string }>(\"GET\", \"/api/health\");\n      if (!h.ok) throw new Error(\"health ok=false\");\n      step(\"health\", `version ${h.version ?? \"?\"}`);\n      break;\n    } catch (e) {\n      if (i >= HEALTH_TRIES) throw e;\n      console.log(`… health  attempt ${i} failed (${(e as Error).message}), retrying`);\n      await sleep(3_000);\n    }\n  }\n\n  const session = await call<{ demoUrl: string }>(\"GET\", \"/api/session\");\n  if (!cookie) throw new Error(\"GET /api/session set no __Host-ws cookie\");\n  step(\"session\", \"workspace cookie set\");\n\n  await call(\"POST\", \"/api/demo/mode\", { modes: [\"healthy\"] });\n  step(\"demo\", `healthy at ${session.demoUrl}`);\n\n  const { id } = await call<{ id: string }>(\"POST\", \"/api/incidents?smoke=rule\", { message: \"diagnose demo\" });\n  step(\"incident\", id);\n\n  const deadline = Date.now() + RUN_TIMEOUT_MS;\n  let snap: Snapshot;\n  do {\n    await sleep(POLL_MS);\n    snap = await call<Snapshot>(\"GET\", `/api/incidents/${id}`);\n  } while (snap.activeRunId !== null && Date.now() < deadline);\n  if (snap.activeRunId !== null) throw new Error(`the run didn't finish within ${RUN_TIMEOUT_MS / 1000} s`);\n  if (snap.category !== \"healthy\") throw new Error(`expected category healthy, got ${snap.category} (status ${snap.status})`);\n  step(\"diagnosis\", `healthy, ${snap.mode}, incident ${snap.status}`);\n}\n\nmain().then(\n  () => console.log(`smoke passed: ${base}`),\n  (e: unknown) => {\n    console.error(`✗ smoke failed: ${e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)}`);\n    process.exit(1);\n  },\n);\n"
}

36	## 2. Stack and versions
37	
38	| Concern | Choice |
39	|---|---|
40	| Package manager and layout | pnpm with a single package. The main Worker and React client are built by **Vite with `@cloudflare/vite-plugin` 1.64**. `demo-service/` is a second Worker, included through `auxiliaryWorkers` so the Service Binding works in `vite dev`. |
41	| Runtime | `wrangler` 4.151, `compatibility_date: "2026-10-01"`, flags `nodejs_compat` and `global_fetch_strictly_public` (lets probes reach our own `workers.dev` demo without error 1042, and keeps fetch on the public internet) |
42	| Server | `agents` 0.28.1 (pinned), `hono` 4.13 for `/api/*`, `zod` 4.6 |
43	| Client | React 19, `wouter` 3 (routes `/` and `/i/:id`), `agents/react` `useAgent`, `@cloudflare/kumo` 2.14 (exact pin) plus its `echarts` 6 peer, Tailwind v4, `@phosphor-icons/react`, `@fontsource-variable/hanken-grotesk` and `@fontsource-variable/jetbrains-mono` |
44	| Tooling | TypeScript 6.0 (`typescript-eslint` needs < 6.1), ESLint 10 with `typescript-eslint`. `Env` is generated by `wrangler types` from both wrangler configs. |
45	| Tests | `vitest` 4.1 with **`@cloudflare/vitest-plugin` 1.4** (`cloudflareTest()`; this is the renamed `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers`, which is deprecated). Three Vitest projects: `unit` (node), `integration` (main Worker), `demo` (demo service). See §14. |
46	| LLM | Workers AI `@cf/openai/gpt-oss-120b` (primary) and `@cf/zai-org/glm-4.7-flash` (fallback) through AI Gateway `incident-copilot`. Voice uses `@cf/openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo`. |
47	
48	## 3. Architecture
49	
50	```
51	Browser (React + Kumo) ──HTTPS /api/*──▶ Main Worker (Hono) ──▶ D1
52	        │                                  │  ├─ getAgentByName(IncidentAgent, id).init()
53	        └──WS /agents/incident-agent/<id>──▶ IncidentAgent DO (SQLite)  ◀─ RPC ─┐
54	                                              │ runWorkflow / approveWorkflow   │
55	                                              ▼                                 │
56	                                       DiagnosisWorkflow (AgentWorkflow) ───────┘
57	                                              │ env.AI (via AI Gateway) · tools → internet
58	                                              │ env.DEMO (Service Binding → DemoAdmin RPC)
59	Cron (every 1 min) ─▶ scheduled(): due watches → quick check → WatchWorkflow (on fail)
60	WatchWorkflow ─▶ IncidentAgent.init(watch) · NotifyWorkflow ─▶ Slack/Discord
61	Demo Worker: public /w/<ws>/… (reads DemoState DO) · DemoAdmin WorkerEntrypoint (binding only)
62	```
63	
64	| Component | Owns | Doesn't own |
65	|---|---|---|
66	| Main Worker `worker/index.ts` | Routing order: `routeAgentRequest`, then Hono `/api/*`, then assets. Workspace cookie, target parsing, quotas, voice, `scheduled()`. | Incident state |
67	| `IncidentAgent` | The source of truth for one incident: SQL tables, synced `IncidentState`, connection roles, transitions, narration, approvals, monitor ticks, postmortem, D1 index sync | Long tool loops (those belong to the Workflow) |
68	| `DiagnosisWorkflow` | One bounded run: planning turns, tool steps, findings, narration step, approval wait, action, verify | Monitoring after verify (D11) |
69	| `WatchWorkflow` | Confirm 2 of 3 failures, then open an incident, start diagnosis and trigger notify | Quick checks (cron does those) |
70	| `NotifyWorkflow` | One retried step that sends a webhook (D18) | |
71	| Demo Worker | Public demo pages per workspace and mode, `DemoState` DO, `DemoAdmin` RPC | |
72	
73	**Routing:** `run_worker_first: ["/api/*", "/agents/*", "/w/*"]` (`/w/*` for the local demo pass-through, §7.5), `not_found_handling: "single-page-application"`.
74

107	## 5. Configuration
108	
109	### 5.1 `wrangler.jsonc` (main)
110	
111	```jsonc
112	{
113	  "name": "incident-copilot",
114	  "main": "worker/index.ts",
115	  "compatibility_date": "2026-10-01",
116	  "compatibility_flags": ["nodejs_compat", "global_fetch_strictly_public"],
117	  "assets": { "not_found_handling": "single-page-application", "run_worker_first": ["/api/*", "/agents/*", "/w/*"] },
118	  "observability": { "enabled": true },
119	  "ai": { "binding": "AI" },
120	  "d1_databases": [{ "binding": "DB", "database_name": "incident-copilot", "database_id": "<from terraform output>", "migrations_dir": "migrations" }],
121	  "durable_objects": { "bindings": [{ "name": "IncidentAgent", "class_name": "IncidentAgent" }] },
122	  "migrations": [{ "tag": "v1", "new_sqlite_classes": ["IncidentAgent"] }],
123	  "workflows": [
124	    { "name": "diagnosis", "binding": "DIAGNOSIS_WORKFLOW", "class_name": "DiagnosisWorkflow" },
125	    { "name": "watch",     "binding": "WATCH_WORKFLOW",     "class_name": "WatchWorkflow" },
126	    { "name": "notify",    "binding": "NOTIFY_WORKFLOW",    "class_name": "NotifyWorkflow" }
127	  ],
128	  "services": [{ "binding": "DEMO", "service": "incident-copilot-demo", "entrypoint": "DemoAdmin" }],
129	  "ratelimits": [
130	    { "name": "PROBE_LIMIT", "namespace_id": "1001", "simple": { "limit": 10, "period": 60 } },
131	    { "name": "RUN_LIMIT",   "namespace_id": "1002", "simple": { "limit": 3,  "period": 60 } },
132	    { "name": "VOICE_LIMIT", "namespace_id": "1003", "simple": { "limit": 5,  "period": 60 } }
133	  ],
134	  "triggers": { "crons": ["* * * * *"] },
135	  "vars": { "AI_GATEWAY_ID": "incident-copilot", "DEMO_BASE_URL": "https://incident-copilot-demo.<sub>.workers.dev", "LLM_MODE": "live" }
136	}
137	```
138	
139	- **Secrets:** `WEBHOOK_KEY` (base64, 32 bytes), set with `wrangler secret put`.
140	- **Local:** `.dev.vars` sets `LLM_MODE=mock`, plus `DEMO_BASE_URL` and `SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN` = `http://localhost:5173` for the local demo (§7.5).
141	- **Demo wrangler:** `name: incident-copilot-demo`, a `DemoState` DO with a `new_sqlite_classes` migration, and `WorkerEntrypoint` `DemoAdmin` exported alongside the default fetch handler.
142	
143	### 5.2 Constants (`worker/loop/budget.ts`, `safety/*`)
144	
145	| Constant | Value |
146	|---|---|
147	| `MAX_TOOL_CALLS` | 6 |
148	| `MAX_PLAN_TURNS` | 3 (plus 1 narrate turn, so 4 LLM turns total) |
149	| `RUN_DEADLINE_MS` | 60 000 |
150	| `SUBREQUEST_BUDGET` | 45. Counts every outbound fetch, every LLM call **and** every Agent RPC or D1 call from the Workflow. Worst-case cost is reserved before each tool runs. *WP-02:* the Workers limits page lists "subrequests per invocation: 50" and a separate "subrequests to internal services: 1,000" on Free, but its definition of a subrequest also names "Cloudflare services like R2, KV, or D1", and Workflows count per instance. This wasn't verified live (no deploy, by choice), so the conservative count stays. |
151	| `HTTP_SAMPLES` | 3 |
152	| `MAX_HOPS` | 5 |
153	| `READ_CAP` | 16 KB |
154	| `SNIPPET_TO_LLM` | 500 chars |
155	| `FETCH_TIMEOUT` | 10 s |
156	| `CRT_READ_CAP` | 256 KB |
157	| `CRT_MAX_ENTRIES` | 20 |
158	| `CRT_CACHE_TTL` | 1 h (Cache API) |
159	| `EVIDENCE_DIGEST_MAX` | 1.5 KB per check (this is what moves between steps and goes to the LLM) |
160	| `HIGH_LATENCY_MS` | median total ≥ 2000 ms or median TTFB ≥ 1500 ms |
161	| `LLM_TIMEOUT_MS` | 25 000 per LLM call. A timeout counts as a failure, so the fast fallback answers (*WP-07*) |

163	| `BURST_TICK_S` | 60 × 15 |

## 1. Overview

Incident Copilot is an AI on-call assistant that runs on Cloudflare. An engineer describes a symptom in chat or by voice, for example "api.example.com is slow" or "checkout.example.com is throwing errors". An AI agent decides which diagnostics to run, runs them as durable, retryable workflow steps, and explains in plain English what is wrong, citing the evidence. It then proposes a next step, which runs only after the engineer approves it.

It can also watch a few endpoints on a schedule. When a watched endpoint fails, it confirms the failure, opens an incident, investigates on its own and notifies the engineer. The diagnosis is already waiting when they arrive.

**Target user:** one engineer debugging a public endpoint they're responsible for.

**Job to be done:**
> *"Tell me in plain English why this endpoint is unhealthy, show me the evidence, and suggest the next step."*

**Why this product:** first-response triage of an unhealthy endpoint is repetitive, manual work that people have learned to live with: run `dig`, run `curl -v`, check the cert, check the provider's status page, then piece it together. That's an operational problem that can be solved with software, and it's a natural fit for Cloudflare's network, its 1.1.1.1 resolver and durable workflows.

---

--
### 7.6 Action catalog (`actions/catalog.ts`)

| ID | Args | Proposable when | Execute | Verify |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `restore_demo_service` | `{}` | Target host = demo host, path starts with `/w/<incident.workspaceId>/`, category ≠ `healthy` | `env.DEMO.setModes(ws, ["healthy"])` (clears every mode) | `quickCheck` pass, then `afterAction` |
| `add_to_watchlist` | `{intervalSec: 300}` | Not already watched and under caps | Insert into `watches` | None; outcome goes on the timeline |
| `recheck_burst` | `{}` | No monitor active | Agent `monitor(kind:"burst")` | Ticks |
| `generate_postmortem` | `{}` | status = `resolved` | `generatePostmortem()` | None |

The Workflow drops any action that isn't proposable. At most one action per run.

*As built (WP-12), `worker/actions/catalog.ts`:*
- `proposable(action, ctx)` is pure. `restore_demo_service` needs:
  - the target to be **this workspace's** demo page (`isOwnDemo`: the same origin as `DEMO_BASE_URL`, and a path under `/w/<workspaceId>/`);
  - a category that isn't `healthy`;
  - a status of `investigating` or `awaiting_approval` (§10 has no edge from `mitigated` or `resolved` to `awaiting_approval`).

  The other three actions return false until their WPs (18, 22, 24).
- **Rule merge (user decision):** `pickAction` takes the model's `actions`, then the rule actions (`restore_demo_service` for any non-healthy category), removes duplicates, and uses the first proposable one. A broken demo therefore always gets the card, even when the model leaves it out. In WP-11's live runs it did leave it out for slow, redirect loop and bad caching.
- The planning prompt says "This target is the user's own demo service" when `RunContext.isDemo` is true.
- `execute` for restore calls `env.DEMO.setModes(ws, ["healthy"])`. `ACTION_TITLES` gives the card titles.
